Um, it started right after I graduated high school... I had just started taking my college classes and working full-time at the clinic, and that's when these feelings really set in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, like jogging, knitting, cooking, or just reading after my classes. But now... I just come home and sit on my bed in my scrubs. I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you do, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not even a little bit. Like I was saying, I used to do all those things... jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... they used to help me relax and feel good. But now there's just zero pleasure in any of it. I really just come home, sit on my bed in my work clothes, and feel nothing towards the stuff I used to love. I just don't care anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself. But I do keep having thoughts like... it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Or if I could just disappear, so I wouldn't mess anything up again.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the past two months where you felt significantly better for at least a couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... it's just been pretty much exactly like this the whole time. I haven't had any breaks where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities as well?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's kind of like what I was saying earlier. I used to clear my head by jogging, or knitting, or reading after class, and it's exactly the same with social stuff now. I just end up sitting on my bed in my scrubs, and I really just can't make myself care about going out or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt consistently depressed for a long time, and then it went away for a while before coming back?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've felt this down for this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this depressed in your life?\n\nPatient: No, just this once. Um, like I was just saying, this is really the first time it's ever been this bad. So, no, I haven't had any other times like this before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your depression is getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it getting worse, exactly. It's just kind of... stuck. Like, it's been pretty much the same constant feeling every day since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think I've lost some weight. I just... I forget to eat until late in the day now. And when I do remember, I usually just force down some crackers or have some coffee. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything, but I've noticed my work pants have been getting pretty loose on me lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But now I just go home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your depressive feelings went away completely for at least two months before returning?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. There hasn't been a break like that. This has been going on for about a year and two months straight. It all started right after I graduated high school, right when I began my college classes and started working full-time at the clinic. It's just been there ever since.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least two months since your depressive feelings started?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, there hasn't been any break like that. I haven't felt back to my normal self for even a few days, let alone two whole months. It's just been constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your depressive feelings change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's just been the same heavy feeling ever since it started, no matter what time of year it is.\n\nDoctor: Has your current episode of depression lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been two years. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's been about a year and two months. It all just started after I graduated high school and began my college classes and working full-time at the clinic.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, it started right after I graduated high school... I had just started taking my college classes and working full-time at the clinic, and that's when these feelings really set in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, like jogging, knitting, cooking, or just reading after my classes. But now... I just come home and sit on my bed in my scrubs. Like I was saying, I used to do all those things... jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... they used to help me relax and feel good. But now there's just zero pleasure in any of it. I really just come home, sit on my bed in my work clothes, and feel nothing towards the stuff I used to love. I just don't care anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself. But I do keep having thoughts like... it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Um... it's just been pretty much exactly like this the whole time. I haven't had any breaks where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities as well?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's kind of like what I was saying earlier. I used to clear my head by jogging, or knitting, or reading after class, and it's exactly the same with social stuff now. I just end up sitting on my bed in my scrubs, and I really just can't make myself care about going out or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt consistently depressed for a long time, and then it went away for a while before coming back?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. I came in because... well, I just can't stop feeling like I'm a danger and a disappointment. I had this one near-miss at work—I'm a nurse—and it just feels like that incident proves I'm not meant to be a nurse at all. I just keep having this feeling that everyone would be better off without me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about being a danger and a disappointment?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a year and two months now. Um, it started right after I graduated high school... I had just started taking my college classes and working full-time at the clinic, and that's when these feelings really set in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, like jogging, knitting, cooking, or just reading after my classes. But now... I just come home and sit on my bed in my scrubs. I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you do, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not even a little bit. Like I was saying, I used to do all those things... jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... they used to help me relax and feel good. But now there's just zero pleasure in any of it. But I do keep having thoughts like... it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Um... it's just been pretty much exactly like this the whole time. I haven't had any breaks where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities as well?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I just end up sitting on my bed in my scrubs, and I really just can't make myself care about going out or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt consistently depressed for a long time, and then it went away for a while before coming back?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days before, like anyone, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've felt this down for this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this depressed in your life?\n\nPatient: No, just this once. Um, like I was just saying, this is really the first time it's ever been this bad. So, no, I haven't had any other times like this before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your depression is getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it getting worse, exactly. It's just kind of... stuck. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything, but I've noticed my work pants have been getting pretty loose on me lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find pleasure in only slightly, rather than none at all?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it's pretty much none at all. Um, I used to do a lot of things to clear my head after class... like jogging, knitting, cooking, or reading. But now I just go home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your depressive feelings went away completely for at least two months before returning?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. There hasn't been a break like that. This has been going on for about a year and two months straight. It's just been there ever since.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least two months since your depressive feelings started?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, there hasn't been any break like that. I haven't felt back to my normal self for even a few days, let alone two whole months. It's just been constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your depressive feelings change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's just been the same heavy feeling ever since it started, no matter what time of year it is.\n\nDoctor: Has your current episode of depression lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been two years. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's been about a year and two months. It all just started after I graduated high school and began my college classes and working full-time at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your depressive feelings lessened for at least two weeks since they started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It hasn't really let up at all since it started. Definitely not for a stretch of two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression will ever get better?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... I'd like to think it will, but it's hard to see how when it's just been this constant heavy feeling for over a year now. It just feels like this is how things are going to be.\n\nDoctor: Has your depression lasted for two years or less?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's less than two years. Like I mentioned a few minutes ago, it's been about a year and two months.
